        "date": "2026-10-15",
        "authors": "Bowen Yang, Kaiming Jin, Zichen Ding et al.",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22234",

        "summary": "Real desktop automation is neither purely visual nor purely programmatic. Agents must understand user-facing interfaces while efficiently manipulating files, scripts, and system state. However, native GUI/code coordination cannot be obtained by prompt-level tool composition. In our preliminary experiments, naively adding a code tool degrades a GUI agent, revealing that GUI/code routing must be internalized through training on high-quality hybrid data. We present OS-Helix, an end-to-end trained multimodal computer-using agent that unifies GUI actions and code execution in one action space. To construct the required supervision, we introduce application-artifact composition, pairing desktop applications with authentic file artifacts to instantiate executable Ubuntu states, and a decoupled instruction/evaluator generation pipeline that produces environment-grounded tasks whose final states can be verified regardless of the solution modality. We cold-start the policy with SFT on filtered hybrid data and further improve it through online RL in live desktop environments. For RL, we propose Step-balanced GSPO, which aggregates importance weight by action step rather than raw assistant tokens to prevent long code actions from dominating heterogeneous-action optimization. On OSWorld-Verified, OS-Helix-27B and OS-Helix-35B-A3B achieve relative improvements of 35.0% and 33.7% over their matched Qwen3.5 hybrid baselines, while OS-Helix-27B-RL further improves by 42.9% to reach 66.44% success. Ablations provide evidence consistent with contributions from learned GUI/code coordination, scalable verifiable supervision, and action step balanced optimization, while demonstrating cross-platform transfer on WindowsAgentArena. Our project homepage is at https://os-copilot.github.io/OS-Helix/."

        "title": "Why Generate a Click? Plug-in Decision Heads for Web Agents",
        "date": "2026-10-14",
        "authors": "Chengguang Gan, Yunhao Liang, Hanjun Wei et al.",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20107",
        "id": "2610.20107",
        "summary": "Web agents built on vision language models decide every action by generating text such as click(12), although most of that text encodes a choice among a few action types and a few dozen marked elements. We read these choices directly from the hidden states that the generator computes while it reads the goal and the screenshot. Plug-in decision heads on a frozen generator predict the action type, pool the visual tokens inside each numbered box to pick the target, and point at a listed text option or at a WRITE slot that leaves the writing of new text to the generator. A confidence cascade executes the heads' action when they are sure and otherwise lets the generator decode from the KV cache of the same forward pass. With Qwen3-VL 4B and 8B generators trained on 213k web steps, a strict threshold keeps the cascade between 0.3 points below and 1.4 points above the generator on seven offline test sets while the heads decide 12 to 64% of the steps. A looser threshold raises step success on unseen Mind2Web websites by 3.1 and 1.8 points, costs up to 2 points on GUIAct and MiniWoB++, and lifts WebArena task success from 12.5 to 13.8% (4B) and from 13.6 to 14.6% (8B) with less than half of the generated tokens. Training the heads jointly with the generator lowers the generator's own step success by up to 4.9 points; heads on a frozen generator avoid this cost."

        "title": "How GUI Agents Fail: GraceBench for Execution Stability Diagnosis",
        "date": "2026-10-14",
        "authors": "Ridong Zhang, Shuxian Liang, Chaoqun Wan et al.",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19900",
        "id": "2610.19900",
        "summary": "As GUI agents are increasingly deployed for mobile task automation, their reliability depends not only on final task outcomes, but also on whether they reach these outcomes through stable interaction trajectories. In automation workflows, unnecessary actions, detours, and execution errors can add noise to collected data, introduce irreversible side effects, or undermine trust, even when the agent eventually completes the task. It is therefore important to systematically diagnose the execution stability, including whether an agent deviates from a feasible path, enters a loop, becomes stagnant, or recovers from unstable actions. Existing benchmarks, however, primarily assess final task success or a small set of intermediate milestones, leaving step-level execution behavior unclear. To bridge this gap, we introduce GraceBench, an online evaluation framework that represents the reachable interaction space of mobile applications as UI Transition Graphs (UTGs), providing a structural reference for step-level execution diagnosis. To support reliable alignment between dynamic UI observations and graph states, GraceBench uses video-grounded trajectory evaluation over this graph-aligned representation, assessing process stability beyond final success with metrics such as deviation rate, loop burden rate, and recovery ability. GraceBench contains 169 tasks spanning 10 applications and constructs 10 UTGs with 2,909 canonical GUI states and 3,116 observed transitions. Across 3,718 trajectories, GraceBench shows that outcome scores hide execution instability: GPT-5.6 Sol achieves the highest success rate (SR) but the lowest recovery ability, 51.24% of successful runs still contain a deviation or loop, and trajectories with the same success rate (SR) and completion rate (CR) can differ substantially in recovery behavior. Code and data will be released on https://iaie-physicalai.github.io/GraceBench."
      }

        "title": "Adaptive-Group Process Rewards for GUI Agents",
        "date": "2026-10-13",
        "authors": "Zirui Shang, Yang Liu, Xin Shu et al.",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17710",
        "id": "2610.17710",
        "summary": "Training graphical user interface (GUI) agents with reinforcement learning suffers from sparse outcome rewards, leading to inefficient learning. Process rewards offer a promising direction for credit assignment to intermediate actions; however, accurately evaluating an action requires identifying evidence across long-horizon GUI trajectories, as its value may depend on information beyond its immediate state and observation. In this paper, we propose an adaptive-group process reward framework that dynamically partitions a GUI trajectory into semantically coherent subgoal groups, enabling process reward estimation at both the action and group levels. At the action level, a critic evaluates each action based on the visual changes and subsequent actions within the group for action correctness and contribution to the subgoal. At the group level, the framework aggregates action-level evaluations to assess subgoal completion and contribution to the overall task, and further refines these assessments by leveraging the context of all groups in the trajectory. The process reward of each action is obtained by combining its action-level evaluation with the refined group-level evaluation. We further augment GRPO's outcome advantage with the proposed process rewards to provide finer-grained guidance for GUI agent training. Experiments on process reward benchmarks and downstream RL training demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework."

        "title": "Towards Safe Autonomy for Computer Use",
        "date": "2026-10-13",
        "authors": "Dominik Seip, Bartol Markovinović, Matthias Hein",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18508",
        "id": "2610.18508",
        "summary": "The safety of LLM-based agents currently rests on alignment training and safety classifiers, neither of which can rule out that a sophisticated indirect prompt injection (IPI) goes through and causes significant harm. Recent work therefore builds agents with information-flow guarantees that rely on structured tool interfaces to interact with the environment. Computer Use Agents (CUAs) act visually on the computer screen, which does not provide a boundary between trusted first-party interfaces and untrusted, potentially attacker-authored content. Actions are equally ambiguous: a click may land on an application toolbar or a button controlled by an attacker. Treating the user as the source of trust and confirming each sensitive decision produces confirmation fatigue, with the risk of missing harmful actions. We introduce SA-CUA, a dual-LLM framework that generates a reliable trust boundary for CUAs by deriving conservative deterministic rules from the accessibility tree for unmasking trusted regions from the privileged LLM's screenshot, keeping trusted and untrusted content separated. Human interaction is only required for proper task specification in the beginning. During the task a robust endorsement channel allows values to pass from the untrusted to the trusted domain by verifying information via trusted channels. This allows the agent to autonomously handle tasks involving untrusted information. SA-CUA maintains much higher utility than CaMeL-CUA while keeping the attack success rate at zero."

        "title": "Interaction-as-a-Judge",

        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17895",
        "id": "2610.17895",
        "summary": "Coding agents are tasked with producing sophisticated interactive applications such as websites, desktop applications, and games. We demonstrate that Interaction-as-a-Judge can improve both evaluation and generation of interactive applications. To this end, we collect and release two complementary datasets: $6,858$ pairs of applications with human preferences and corresponding agent traces, and $222$ applications with human-verified requirement labels. Relative to standard non-interactive approaches, we find that Interaction-as-a-Judge improves evaluation in terms of predicting human preferences ($65.6\\% \\to 69.1\\%$) and requirement verification ($77.0\\% \\to 81.6\\%$). We also observe scaling benefits: across both preference alignment and requirement verification, higher-quality interactions improve performance. Finally, through both inference- and training-time experiments, we demonstrate that Interaction-as-a-Judge can also improve the quality of generated artifacts. At inference time, Interaction-as-a-Judge repairs $75.5\\%$ of broken requirements within five attempts, compared to $55.4\\%$ for code-only self-feedback. At training time, using Interaction-as-a-Judge as a reward signal improves win-rate over the base model by $6.7$pp while reducing both generated code length by $35.7\\%$ and build failure rates by $7.2$pp compared to a code-only reward signal."

        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17792",

        "summary": "GUI grounding enables web agents to translate natural-language instructions into actionable interface locations.         "date": "2026-10-12",
        "authors": "Zian Yuan, Wei Li",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16778",
        "id": "2610.16778",
        "summary": "Cloud graphical user interface (GUI) agents send instructions and screenshots to remote planners, exposing personally identifiable information (PII). On AndroidLab, PrivacyGuard achieves the highest or tied-highest task-success point estimate among protection pipelines for all four planners.         "title": "Seg2Click: Learning Pixel-Level Action Masks for GUI Visual Grounding",
        "date": "2026-10-12",
        "authors": "Jingchao Wang, Dingjiang Huang",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16234",
        "id": "2610.16234",
        "summary": "Visual grounding is a central challenge for GUI agents, requiring an instruction to be mapped to a target within a crowded screenshot. Coordinate generation with single-point supervision reduces a spatially extended target to one position, despite the presence of many valid click locations. To characterize reliability throughout development, we integrate trajectory performance across user-tolerance thresholds rather than impose a fixed stopping criterion. Experiments across open- and closed-source frontier models show that EvoWeb reveals clear performance differences between models, with cross-request integration emerging as a key challenge. Further analysis shows that later requests with deeper or more numerous dependencies are solved less accurately, while runtime errors become more frequent over successive requests. EvoWeb provides a testbed for evaluating and improving coding agents in long-horizon, multi-request development."
      },
      {
        "title": "HyWe: Unifying Search and Operation for Real-World Hybrid Web Tasks",
        "date": "2026-10-12",
        "authors": "Zijian Cai, Haowei Liu, Kailai Chen et al.",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16319",
        "id": "2610.16319",
        "summary": "Real-world hybrid web tasks need both information gathering and page interaction, yet search-based and operation-based agents are developed and evaluated separately. To bridge this gap, we introduce HyWe, a framework that unifies both capabilities within one model and trajectory. Its two modes use dedicated prompts and tool sets to sustain sub-goal execution; explicit switching selects the needed capability, and shared history lets each mode build on the other's findings. To provide demonstrations of these behaviors, we develop HyWeCollector, a fully automatic pipeline that uses task prototypes, four switching patterns, and multi-agent synthesis to produce verified hybrid trajectories. We train HyWe-35B on these demonstrations to unify efficient information gathering and browser interaction in a single 35B web agent. To evaluate hybrid task completion, we introduce HyWeBench: 100 human-written tasks on websites distinct from training, with human-written sub-goal checklists and an outcome-based evaluation pipeline. Hybrid training enables HyWe-35B to achieve 47.0% success on this benchmark, improving on the untrained base model by 19.0 percentage points and outperforming existing search-based and operation-based agents. Even before training, the same base model solves 28.0% of tasks with HyWe, versus 11.0% with all tools under one prompt. Beyond hybrid tasks, HyWe-35B achieves 59.0% and 88.3% success on Online-Mind2Web and WebVoyager, outperforming existing open-source baselines and cutting action cost per successful task by 48.3% and 51.9% compared with Fara-7B."

        "title": "MAI-Gym: An Open All-in-One Platform for GUI Agent Evaluation and RL Training across Mobile and Computer Environments",
        "date": "2026-10-14",
        "authors": "Panrong Tong, Quyu Kong, Pengxiang Li et al.",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20939",
        "id": "2610.20939",
        "summary": "Online reinforcement learning (RL) with verifiable rewards has driven rapid progress in mathematics, coding, and tool use, but scaling this success to GUI agents remains challenging due to the high cost of interactive environments and the scarcity of realistic training tasks. We introduce MAI-Gym, an open all-in-one platform that unifies GUI agent evaluation, data collection, and online RL training across mobile, computer, and web environments. Through a shared rollout pipeline, MAI-Gym ensures consistent agent behavior across evaluation and training, supports ten established benchmarks, and scales to 20,000 parallel environment instances. Beyond scalable infrastructure, MAI-Gym enables automated synthesis of diverse GUI applications and challenging tasks, including resettable mocked apps and coherent cross-app worlds with execution-validated task verifiers. Using MAI-Gym, we train MAI-Gym-9B, a unified 9B agent for mobile and computer use, achieving state-of-the-art performance among open GUI agents under 10B parameters: 78.4% on MobileWorld and 76.2% on OSWorld-Verified. Importantly, RL in a mocked train-ticketing app transfers to its real-world counterpart, improving task success on a phys- ical phone from 28.8% to 48.1%. We release the framework, synthesis skills, mocked apps, 2,000 verified tasks, and the model checkpoint to support repro- ducible research and further advances in scalable GUI agent training."

      {
        "title": "GroundGUI: GUI Grounding by High-Quality Data and Distillation-to-Reinforcement Learning",
        "date": "2026-10-13",
        "authors": "Qingsong Xie, Jiahao Tian, Jiacheng Tang et al.",
        "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19100",
        "id": "2610.19100",
        "summary": "Constructing robust GUI agents relies on multimodal visual grounding: the ability to precisely map natural language intents to actionable on-screen components. Despite abundant desktop, web, and mobile benchmarks, high-fidelity resources tailored for diverse environments are surprisingly scarce. To bridge this gap, we introduce the Screen Referring Grounding Dataset (ScreenRef), a comprehensive GUI grounding dataset consisting of GUI screenshots paired with natural language referring expressions and box or point annotations. Spanning desktop, web, and mobile domains, ScreenRef comprises 586K screenshots and over 633K referring expression instances, covering tasks such as text recognition, visual matching, spatial understanding, and refusal scenarios. To boost the model's grounding performance, we propose the Distillation-to-Reinforcement Learning (D2RL) method to address the limited generalization of supervised fine-tuning and the sparse reward problem in reinforcement learning. Utilizing ScreenRef, we train Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct via sequential SFT and D2RL training, yielding GroundGUI-8B. GroundGUI-8B achieves 95.2% on ScreenSpot-V2, 63.9% on ScreenSpot-Pro, 65.4% on OSWorld-G, 86.8% on MMBench-GUI L2, and 38.3% on UI-Vision. Among 8B-scale models, it produces substantial gains over models trained on open-source datasets, with performance comparable or even superior to models trained on closed-source data. Code, models, and datasets are available at https://github.com/PrentisAI/GroundGUI."

本周（2026-10-12～10-18，周一至周日）arXiv 上与 browser agent 直接相关的论文共 24 篇（检索式 7 条，扫描 406 条记录，按 arXiv id 去重后落在本周，另有 3 篇 10-10/10-11 的边界条目已在落库表中但未计入本周清单）。整体看，这一周的主线已经从“做得成”转向 成本控制、执行可控性与安全边界：一半以上的工作在解决推理开销、错误归因和注入防护，而不是刷点成功率。

---

一、开源全栈与训练基础设施

  1. OpenGCUA: Toward a Fully Open Stack for Generalist Computer Use（2026-10-15）—— 首个开源“通用 computer-use”全栈，把代码执行纳入动作空间并提出 harness-to-model 决策转移；OSWorld-v2 上 8 个模型（含 GPT-6 Astra、Claude Opus 5）平均部分分 +19.6，每任务步骤减少 53.0%[^1]
  2. MAI-Gym: An Open All-in-One Platform for GUI Agent Evaluation and RL Training（2026-10-14）—— 评测/数据/在线 RL 一套 rollout 管线打通，支持 2 万并行环境；训出的 MAI-Gym-9B 在 MobileWorld 78.4%、OSWorld-Verified 76.2%，且模拟购票 app 上的 RL 迁移到真机把成功率从 28.8% 提到 48.1%[^2]
  3. GroundGUI: GUI Grounding by High-Quality Data and Distillation-to-Reinforcement Learning（2026-10-13）—— 发布 586K 截图 / 633K 指代表达的 ScreenRef 数据集与 D2RL 训练法，8B 模型达 ScreenSpot-V2 95.2%、ScreenSpot-Pro 63.9%，接近闭源数据训练水平[^3]

二、推理成本与效率

  1. LEASH: Uncertainty-Aware Weak-to-Strong Escalation and Oversight for Computer-Use Agents（2026-10-15）—— 训练无关：用弱模型隐状态与历史成功/失败轨迹的相似度当不确定性信号，决定何时升级到强模型；OSWorld 上同升级率下比随机升级高 6.55–11.94 分，Qwen3.5-35B-A3B 达 73.66% 成功率、$0.4117/任务，追平常开 Claude Opus 5（72.90%）而推理成本低 19.2%[^4]
  2. Why Generate a Click? Plug-in Decision Heads for Web Agents（2026-10-14）—— 不再让模型“生成” click(12)，而是在冻结生成器上挂决策头，从同一次前向的隐状态直接读动作类型与目标框；严格阈值下 12–64% 的步骤由头决定且精度持平，WebArena 任务成功率 12.5→13.8%（4B）、13.6→14.6%（8B），生成 token 不到一半[^5]
  3. STP-GUI: Spatially-Aware Token Pruning for Efficient Web-GUI Agents（2026-10-14）—— 指出通用 token 剪枝会把小控件切碎，改成窗口级空间连贯选择；只保留 21% token 仍保住 77.7% 全量性能，延迟 -31%、FLOPs -57%[^6]
  4. Teaching Agents in their Own Style: Blackbox Teacher Distillation for Web Agents（2026-10-14）—— 让黑盒教师当“批改者”，只从首个失误点起用学生自己的风格做最小改写；TimeWarp 上比行为克隆、结果奖励 RL 和教师改写基线高 5.5–10.7 分，并迁移到 OnlineMind2Web[^7]

三、执行可控性、稳定性与失败诊断

  1. Task-State Governance: A Virtual Machine for Verifiable and Recoverable GUI Agent Execution（2026-10-14）—— TaskVM 把任务写成“可编辑契约”（目标 + 必须保持不变的事实 + 观测状态），每个动作标注可逆/可补偿/不可逆；在任务结果持平的前提下提升了契约合规率与用户恢复控制感[^8]
  2. How GUI Agents Fail: GraceBench for Execution Stability Diagnosis（2026-10-14）—— 用 UI 迁移图做步级诊断，提出偏离率、循环负担率、恢复能力三个指标；3,718 条轨迹显示 51.24% 的成功执行里仍含偏离或循环，GPT-5.6 Sol 成功率最高却恢复能力最低[^9]
  3. Reading Right, Acting Wrong: Diagnosing Decision Failures of System One Models in Agent Loops（2026-10-13）—— 给每个决策配事实探针后发现：17 个快速系统的错误动作大多是“读对了但组合错了”，且越准的系统这种错误占比越高，误差稳定聚集在同一批状态上[^10]
  4. Interaction-as-a-Judge（2026-10-13）—— 让 CUA 真的去点应用来当评审：预测人类偏好 65.6%→69.1%、需求核验 77.0%→81.6%；5 次内修复 75.5% 的断裂需求（纯代码自反馈为 55.4%），当奖励信号时胜率 +6.7pp 且代码长度 -35.7%[^11]

四、动作空间、Grounding 与记忆

  1. OS-Helix: Unifying GUI Interaction and Code Execution in Native Computer-Using Agents（2026-10-15）—— 端到端训练把 GUI 动作与代码执行压进同一动作空间；作者前置实验发现“提示层加个代码工具”反而会让 GUI agent 退化，说明路由必须内化进模型[^12]
  2. Player-1: Learning to Play from Synthetic Experience（2026-10-14）—— 用 16 个合成 2D/3D 环境 + PPO 从合成经验学游戏控制；Player-1-9B 在 MineDojo-300 比 SFT 提升 27.8 分，38.33% vs 最强基线 12.63%[^13]
  3. Learn from Move: the Next Step for GUI Agents（2026-10-12）—— 提出按“所需证据”分级的 LatentGUIWorld（I₀ 先验 / I₁ 交互揭示 / I₂ 靠动作-响应推断动态），并把移动与按键解耦成 MOVE；LatentLearner-9B 达 50.0%，联合 RL 后自中心 Drag、Ten-Choice 分别到 92.0%/75.3%[^14]
  4. Seg2Click: Learning Pixel-Level Action Masks for GUI Visual Grounding（2026-10-12）—— 把 grounding 重构成“可执行区域”掩码预测再定点击点，ScreenSpot 92.06%、ScreenSpot-v2 94.10%、ScreenSpot-Pro 55.22%，无需 verifier 或放大重看[^15]
  5. MHVF: Training-Free GUI Grounding via Verification-Guided Multi-Hypothesis Search（2026-10-13）—— 训练无关的多假设探索 + 局部放大验证，把 Qwen2.5-VL-7B 在 ScreenSpot-Pro 上从 23.6% 拉到 53.6%[^16]
  6. Learning How to Remember: Memory Modality Selection for GUI Agents（2026-10-13）—— 实证发现“文本记忆还是视觉记忆更好”是步级相关的，SwitchMem 逐步路由，在三个 GUI 基准上稳定优于固定模态记忆[^17]
  7. Adaptive-Group Process Rewards for GUI Agents（2026-10-13）—— 先把轨迹切成语义子目标组，再做动作级 + 组级过程奖励并注入 GRPO 优势，缓解长程 GUI 的稀疏奖励[^18]

五、安全与隐私（本周分量最重的方向之一）

  1. Towards Safe Autonomy for Computer Use（2026-10-13）—— SA-CUA 双 LLM 架构：从 accessibility tree 推出确定性规则，把可信区域从截图中“揭开”，并用人审的 endorsement 通道把不可信值送进可信域；攻击成功率保持 0，效用远高于 CaMeL-CUA，任务开始后基本不需人工确认[^19]
  2. BrowseSafe++: Benchmarking Prompt Injection Detection Across Web Contexts, Languages, and Browser Views（2026-10-12）—— 38,675 对“注入页 + 干净对照页”，每对同时给 HTML、Markdown、AXTree、截图四种视图；0.8B 检测器 F1 超过前沿模型检测器；BrowseSafe-Adapt 还能把原本成功率个位数的注入提到 95.41%[^20]
  3. PrivacyGuard: Source-Linked Sanitization and Locally Resolvable PII References for Cloud GUI Agents（2026-10-12）—— 针对云 GUI agent 上传截图泄漏 PII，做源跨度重建式脱敏 + 会话级可本地解析引用；源值泄漏相对最强基线降 33.55%–82.60%，跨形连续性从 0.04 提到 0.81，AndroidLab 任务成功率仍保持最高或并列最高[^21]

六、任务形态扩展

  1. HyWe: Unifying Search and Operation for Real-World Hybrid Web Tasks（2026-10-12）—— 把检索与页面操作合到一个模型的同一轨迹里（显式切换 + 共享历史）；HyWe-35B 在 HyWeBench 47.0%（比未训练基线 +19 分），Online-Mind2Web 59.0%、WebVoyager 88.3%，单任务动作成本比 Fara-7B 少 48.3%/51.9%[^22]
  2. EvoWeb: A Benchmark for Coding Agents on Evolving Web Applications（2026-10-12）—— 320 条累积迭代请求覆盖 32 个 web 应用，并让多模态 web agent 在真实浏览器里逐请求验收；结果显示“跨请求集成”是核心短板，依赖越深越晚的请求越容易错[^23]
  3. Glasseek: A Transparent Recipe for Training and Trustworthy Evaluation of DeepSearch Agents（2026-10-12）—— 全透明 deep search 配方（数据、权重、流程、代码全开），CM-GSPO 让搜索与摘要共用策略并只优化执行子轨迹；同时首次系统审计了开放网页测试集泄漏，9B agent 在多数 deep research 基准上超过专有前沿模型[^24]

---

可以重点看的三篇（如果只挑三篇）：LEASH 解决“强模型全开太贵”的实际部署问题且给出成本数字；GraceBench 说明成功率高不等于过程可靠，是评测视角的重要纠偏；BrowseSafe++ 提供了目前最贴近真实浏览器四种视图的注入基准，对做 browser agent 落地的团队直接可用。

产物：候选全量已写入 Data 表 arxiv_browser_agent_2026_10_12_2026_10_18（27 行，字段：标题/发布日期/链接/作者/摘要，按链接去重），其中本周 24 篇即上表。

口径与限制：时间窗按检索时刻（2026-10-18）向前 8 天取超集，报告只保留 2026-10-12 及以后；检索式基于摘要/标题短语匹配，摘要未出现 “browser agent / web agent / GUI agent” 等关键词的论文会漏检；所有一句话结论均来自摘要，未读全文与附录数据。
